Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

European TMT gross bond supply may reach €150bn in 2027,
driven by tech funding for AI and data centres rather than
traditional telecoms. Strong cash ﬂows and credit quality
support issuance, but disciplined funding will be key to market
absorption
Jan Frederik Slijkerman
AI and data centre investment is expected to be the main driver of TMT bond
issuance in 2027
We predict another year of strong supply for the European TMT
sector. We expect €150bn of gross issuance and €103bn of net

issuance, given €47bn of debt redemptions. The additional
issuance is not coming from the traditional telecom or media
issuers, but from global tech companies.
As we expect the data centre build-out to continue in 2027, issuers
are likely to return to the European investment-grade credit market
to raise part of the required funding. Meanwhile, we do not see a
major reason for traditional sectors to reduce or increase

Telecoms supply
We will break down the main factors aﬀecting debt issuance,
starting with FY27 capex requirements. While we expect capex as a
percentage of sales to decline, aggregate capex is likely to increase

slightly. Companies such as Orange and Vodafone are integrating
recent acquisitions and may need to invest additional capital to
realise merger synergies. We therefore do not expect the lower
capex-to-sales ratio to translate into less issuance. Nevertheless,
BT and KPN are exceptions, as both clearly intend to reduce capex
in FY27.
Low-single-digit EBITDA growth gives companies some capacity to
issue additional debt while maintaining stable leverage. However,

higher interest rates provide an incentive to keep debt levels in
check. We therefore expect the increase in debt issuance to be
driven mainly by M&A. Several debt-funded acquisitions of smaller
networks could generate around €5bn of net issuance. A further
€3bn could come from US operators raising euro-denominated
funding for spectrum payments in 2027.

### Page 3

Technology supply
In 2027, we believe the AI build-out will continue. Tech companies
currently report healthy returns on investment, demand for AI
services continues to outpace supply, and companies want to
maintain their market positions. We therefore expect further capex
growth in 2027, with the large hyperscalers investing close to
US$1tr in ﬁscal 2027.
As in 2026, we expect these companies to ﬁnance their
investments largely from operating cash ﬂow, but also through

debt issuance, with additional funding coming from European
markets. We expect hyperscaler data centre operators to issue at
least €60bn in 2027, somewhat above the approximately €35bn
issued year-to-date in 2026, including issuance by NTT.
In addition to the €60bn of issuance from hyperscaler data centre
operators, we expect €35bn of net supply from other technology
issuers. Companies are likely to pursue acquisitions and raise
additional funding for investment, including semiconductor,

software and payment services companies. Including €8bn from
telecom issuers, this brings total TMT net supply to €103bn. This
would translate into gross supply of approximately €150bn, which,
in our view, is close to the amount the European market may be
able to absorb.
Supply discipline will be important
Companies will need to issue prudently and provide clear guidance
on future funding plans, as spreads may only tighten once the
market no longer expects substantial new supply. Nevertheless,

current spreads look attractive relative to the issuers’ high ratings,
with much of the widening driven by supply and liquidity eﬀects.
Relatively wide CDS spreads also suggest that technical factors,
rather than a material deterioration in credit risk, are weighing on
valuations. This combination of high credit quality and attractive
spreads should attract new money to the investment-grade
market.

Challenge 1: Does debt issuance reduce
credit quality?
First, we expect the hyperscaler data centre operators Amazon,
Microsoft, Alphabet, Meta and Oracle to grow their combined
EBITDA by US$230bn, or 29%, in FY27, to around US$1tr. Moreover,
with the exception of Oracle, these companies are expected to
operate with limited net debt, with Microsoft holding more cash
than debt. The main caveat is their signiﬁcant lease commitments,
many of which are not yet reﬂected in balance-sheet lease

rating agencies will attach greater weight to these liabilities,
particularly if returns on investment start to decline. In our view, this
is not a material concern for 2027 and may never become one,
given the sector’s strong growth and ability to fund a signiﬁcant
part of investment from operating cash ﬂow. Companies also retain
ﬂexibility to scale back future investment that has not yet been
committed if returns start to disappoint.
Challenge 2: Do credit benchmarks

accommodate more supply?
Do European credit markets have the capacity to absorb additional
supply from technology companies? We think the current share of
technology issuers remains modest. Alphabet accounts for only
1.16% of the iBoxx € Non-Financials index, while Amazon and IBM
account for 0.68% and 0.91%, respectively.
In the US, Oracle’s share of more than 2% in the iBoxx $ Domestic
Non-Financials index illustrates that individual technology issuers

can reach materially higher benchmark weights, while several high-

technology companies have not yet raised funding from European

credit markets.
We therefore see signiﬁcant room for technology companies to
increase issuance in European investment-grade markets. The
inclusion of eligible new bonds in benchmarks also creates a strong
technical incentive for benchmark-aware investors to participate in
new deals, as not owning the bonds could increase their tracking
error.

Europe, USA (%)

Document 345: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

Document 353: GF - PTFE Adoption

Source PDF: all report pdf/GF - PTFE Adoption.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Overseas Technology｜Oct 15,2026 
PTFE Adoption: 
Reshape the High-End Materials Landscape

Overseas  
Technology

Michelle Jing 
SFC CE No. BUK594 
michellejing@gfgroup.com.hk 
Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk

What's new 
Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE board prototype for the first time at OCP Global Summit 2026, highlighting seven supply-chain partners: 
Taiflex (8039 TT), Flexium (6269 TT), TTM Technologies (TTMI US), 3 leading Chinese PCB manufacturers, and 1 leading Chinese CCL 
supplier. We believe this development reinforces our previous view that PTFE remains the most promising material solution for the VR300 
NVL576 switch board, offering the best balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
Comments 
PTFE hybrid remains the preferred solution: As highlighted in our Oct. 5 report, we continue to favor the PTFE hybrid solution for the 
VR300 NVL576 switch board among the various options, given its optimal balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
While pure PTFE remains challenging to process, particularly in lamination and drilling, leading PCB manufacturers have achie ved 
encouraging yields with hybrid structures that combine PTFE signal layers with M9 materials in non-signal layers. 
We like PTFE's long-term adoption potential: PTFE applications in high-frequency and high-speed interconnects could extend beyond 
switch boards. The IEC has published a standard on PTFE-filled CCL, led by the leading Chinese CCL company, highlighting the material's 
relevance to broader circuit-board applications. Our checks also suggest that PTFE materials have entered the testing phase for other 
applications, including compute boards. We therefore remain positive on PTFE's long-term adoption across a broader range of high-end 
PCB applications. 
Limited near-term impact on conventional resin materials, but long-term substitution risks warrant attention: Under the current 
PTFE hybrid design, we expect 8–12 out of 32 layers to adopt PTFE for signal transmission, accounting for less than 50% of the total CCL 
resin content value. Over the longer term, we expect a potential shift toward pure PTFE solutions as improvements in processing 
technology enable commercially viable MP yields, reducing the need for hybrid structures. This could gradually increase substitution risks 
for conventional high-end PCB resin materials, including PPO and HC resin. 
Favor PTFE-exposed suppliers with proven technical and manufacturing capabilities: We remain positive on PTFE-related 
opportunities, particularly suppliers with strong R&D capabilities, proven MP expertise, and established customer qualifications. In CCL, 
we favor Taiflex (8039 TT) and leading Chinese CCL supplier for their PTFE material capabilities and supply-chain exposure. Among the 
five Nvidia-announced PCB suppliers, we favor the leading Chinese PTFE PCB supplier, given its advanced hybrid PTFE processing 
capabilities and encouraging yields. For CCL suppliers that have yet to enter the PTFE supply chain, we recommend monitoring progress 
in developing, while remaining alert to potential market-share losses in their conventional product portfolios. 
Risks  
1) AI demand slowing down; 2) Geo-political risks; 3) Competition.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, held at –0.03% in September,
unchanged from August.

The Leading Index continues to show moderately improved
momentum compared to the middle of the year, with the
latest reads pointing to growth holding at or slightly below
trend heading into late 2026/early 2027. Growth appears
to be stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling. That
said, both the latest fuel prices and the RBA’s September
interest rate increase are still working their way through to
the economy with impacts likely to take some of the gloss off

growth near term. Overall, Westpac expects annual growth
to come in at 1.5% for calendar 2026, a below-trend result.
The Leading Index growth rate has firmed slightly over the
last six months, rising from –0.16% back in March to –0.03%
in September. The component detail shows mixed moves over
the period. Four components have contributed more positively
with reduced drags from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Unemployment Expectations Index (adding +0.23ppts to

the Index growth rate); the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Expectations Index (adding another +0.15ppts);
and the S&P/ASX200 (+0.15ppts) combining with a modest
lift in growth in US industrial production (adding +0.12ppts).
These positives have been partially offset by modest drags
from four previously stable components: dwelling approvals
(–0.34ppts); monthly hours worked (–0.08ppts); commodity
prices, measured in AUD terms (–0.07ppts) and the yield

spread (–0.05ppts).
Overall, the component mix speaks to a lack of direction.
Indeed, despite some large shocks over the last year, the
Leading Index growth rate has been relatively contained since
early 2025, holding in a narrow –0.3% to +0.5% range. This
compares to history which, outside of the COVID and GFC
shocks, has seen growth rates move between a much wider
–1.9% to +1.6% range. Of course, the apparent stability,
which is evident across all components of the index, may not

last but for now it suggests the economy has been stuck in a
bit of a holding pattern over the last two years.
Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
**Please note that this will be the last Westpac press release for
the monthly Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index. We will
continue to publish regular bulletin updates through our westpaciq.
com.au website.

ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_874
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
MIDDLE EAST
BULLETIN
Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.
Q4 2026 forecasts:
Brent crude
US$101/bbl
Japanese LNG
US$24.5/MMBtu

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
attacks on Red Sea shipping keeping Saudi production below
its pre-conflict level until H2 2027. Meanwhile, we look for UAE
production to continue to improve over 2027 and lift above
pre-conflict levels from 2028, as it is no longer constrained by

OPEC quotas. We do not expect a meaningful return of Iranian
oil until an agreement is reached, and damage sustained during
the conflict means production will take time to recover.
We expect global oil demand to be 103.7mb/d in 2026, 0.8mb/d
(0.7%) higher than in 2025, and to rise a further 1.9mb/d (1.8%)
to 105.6mb/d in 2027. Chinese crude imports will continue to
recover over the coming months as refiners restock ahead of
Lunar New Year, but we expect only a gradual return to pre-

conflict levels, as the electrification of transport limits the
recovery in Chinese demand. Globally, demand for diesel and jet
fuel recovers as transport and aviation activity picks up, although
some demand destruction and efficiency gains are expected
to be permanent. The conflict has also highlighted strategic
vulnerabilities, particularly across Asia, so we expect a push to
rebuild reserves adding around 0.5% to demand over 2027.
Fuel prices to ease more slowly than crude

Refined product supply is expected to recover more slowly
than crude, with Gulf refinery runs not returning to pre-war
levels until at least late 2027. Outside the Gulf, there has
been some constructive news. The US has lifted sanctions
on Russian diesel until April 2027, with Russia saying it will
supply more than 4Mt over the coming months, while China
has lifted its ban on refined product exports. However, we
are cautious about how much this will add to the market in

the short term. Much of the Russian supply is headed to the
US and will depend on Russia’s ability to restore refining and
export capacity, while doubts remain over the Russia-Ukraine
energy ceasefire announced by President Trump. China still
manages exports through quotas, which are likely to increase
only gradually as domestic stocks are rebuilt. Refinery runs
should lift ahead of Lunar New Year, but any increase in
exports will take time to reach regional markets.

With product supply recovering slowly and depleted inventories
being rebuilt, Singapore gasoil crack is likely to stay above its
long-run average even as Brent eases, narrowing from US$71/
bbl in Q4 to US$27/bbl by Q4 2027. With Singapore Gas 10ppm
prices, shipping and insurance costs to remain elevated and
easing only gradually in 2027, we forecast Australian diesel
to retail at an average of 274¢/L in Q4, easing to 200¢/L in Q4
2027, and petrol at 230¢/L and 187¢/L respectively.

LNG prices supported through 2027
Japanese LNG prices surged 15.2%mth in September,
averaging US$25.4/MMBtu as supply disruptions in Qatar
and its inability to divert exports around the Strait of Hormuz
tightened markets and intensified competition between
importers. While prices are now more than double pre-conflict
levels, demand destruction across Asia is providing some
offset to tight supply
Our Q4 forecast for Japanese LNG is now US$24.5/mmbtu and

we expect the market to remain structurally tight, with supply
recovery likely to be gradual and prices not returning to pre-
conflict levels until around 2028. Still, Australia’s domestic
gas market should remain insulated, supported by the FY2027
Federal Budget’s domestic gas reservation scheme, which
requires exporters to supply the domestic market with the
equivalent of up to 20% of gas exports. So far this year,
despite the increase in LNG spot prices in Asia, Australian

natural gas prices have remained under the $12 per GJ cap
that has proposed by the Commonwealth government. We are
expecting this to continue well into 2027.
Risks around our oil price outlook are two-sided
Talks are still ongoing in the background, with the US and
Iran exchanging proposals to reopen the Strait, but the gap
between them remains wide. The conflict has also expanded
to the Red Sea and, with inventories sharply drawn down,
there is a smaller buffer to absorb a further shock.

Using the Oxford Economics model, we assess an escalation
scenario, where energy markets face a deeper and more
persistent disruption as the conflict in the Middle East
intensifies. In this case, Brent futures rise to around US$150/
bbl in H1 2027, only dipping to below US$100/bbl in Q2 2028.
We also consider an early resolution scenario, where shipping
and Gulf supply normalise faster, returning Brent to near pre-
conflict levels by end-2027.
Escalation scenario

In the escalation scenario we assume:
Talks stall and the conflict escalates:  the gap between the
US and Iran proves too wide and the US resumes strikes after
the midterms. Iran responds with attacks on shipping and Gulf
energy infrastructure, damaging export, storage and refining
facilities. A lasting agreement is not reached until 2028.
Export routes are disrupted: more frequent attacks curtail
dark crossings of the Strait and ship-to-ship transfers in the

Gulf of Oman. Houthi attacks also cut off Saudi alternative
routes, forcing Gulf producers to cut output and Middle
East crude exports sharply lower. New routes and shipping
arrangements eventually support a recovery in Gulf flows, but
trade patterns take time to adjust.
... and risks remain and while they are two sided ...

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Product supply fails to recover:  The Russia–Ukraine energy
ceasefire does not hold and strikes on Russian refineries
continue, limiting how much diesel Russia can export,
even with the US sanctions waiver extended. China also

reintroduces its export ban to protect domestic supply.
Oil prices lift to around US$150/bbl in H1 2027: With little
buffer to absorb the supply loss, competition for physical
delivery intensifies, with physical prices well above futures
as prompt barrels remain scarce. Higher prices support
supply growth outside OPEC and drive demand destruction,
alongside further emergency stock releases, but a deeper
and more prolonged global deficit remains. Brent stays above

US$100/bbl until mid-2028 as governments rebuild reserves.
The Singapore gasoil crack widens and, with freight and
insurance costs also higher, Australian diesel and petrol prices
average around 247 cpl and 231 cpl at end 2027. Japanese
LNG also remains elevated around US$23.2/MMBtu in Q4
2027, $9.1 above our baseline.
Early resolution scenario
In the early resolution scenario we assume:
A durable agreement: The US and Iran agree terms to reopen
the Strait by the end of year, and, unlike the June interim deal,

the agreement holds, with progress towards a permanent
settlement in early 2027. Attacks on shipping cease and the
US blockade is lifted, allowing Iranian oil back into the market,
initially from storage. Vessels are quickly repositioned to the
Gulf, with freight and war-risk insurance easing from early
2027 rather than mid-year.
A faster recovery in supply:  With export routes secure, Gulf
production and damaged refinery and export capacity recover

sooner than in the baseline. Outside the Gulf, the US extends
its waiver on Russian diesel beyond April 2027 and China
raises export quotas as domestic stocks are rebuilt.
Demand settles below pre-conflict trend: Some demand
destruction and efficiency gains persist, particularly in China.
With inventories rebuilding faster, government restocking is
smaller and more gradual than in the baseline.
Prices fall quickly, but restocking provides a floor:  Brent

Brent futures ease to around US$88/bbl in Q1 2027 as the
premium for prompt physical barrels unwinds. As the market
moves into balance, Brent eases to around US$65/bbl by Q4
2027, close to pre-conflict levels.
Refined product prices ease faster on lower freight costs with
the Singapore gasoil crack narrowing. Australian diesel and
petrol prices fall to around 182 cpl and 170 cpl by Q4 2027. Gas
markets also ease, although damage to Qatar’s gas facility means

export capacity recovers gradually. Japanese LNG falls to around
US$11.9/MMBtu by end-2027, $2.1 below our baseline.
Economic impact
In the escalation scenario, higher fuel costs flow through to
transport, food and other energy-intensive goods such as
plastics and fertilisers. Australian headline CPI remains above
4.5% for much of 2027, with underlying inflation remaining
sticky at above 3% until late 2028. The RBA faces a difficult
trade off between much higher inflation and a significant

hit to activity, as households cut discretionary spending and
businesses defer investment. Higher LNG export earnings
provide only a moderate offset. In the early resolution scenario,
headline inflation is around 0.1ppt below the baseline in 2027.
New Zealand is more exposed in both scenarios, with food
and energy a larger share of its CPI and no offsetting energy
exports. Headline inflation is around 1ppt higher in the
escalation scenario in Q1 2027.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHY CHINA RESISTS REBALANCING ITS GROWTH MODEL?
China has never exported as much as it does now, and its trade surplus has never been higher. At the same time, its
economic growth is slowing, held back by weak domestic demand. The imbalances in China’s growth model, and the ex-
ternal imbalances they give rise to, are therefore continuing to worsen. With Europe taking a tougher line and the United

States, with which China shares nothing more than ‘strategic stability’, the standoff looks set to continue. However, the
European Union and China would have a great deal to lose from a lasting deterioration in their relationship. It is in the
interests of both parties to seek to restore the balance. To this end, it is important to understand Beijing’s objectives and
the constraints facing China. Why does the country resist adjusting its growth model? Whilst exports are a vital asset for

sustaining economic activity and asserting China’s technological and industrial power, this strategy also comes at a cost.
IMBALANCES ARE WORSENING
A K-shaped economy. China’s economic growth slowed to 4.6% year-
on-year (y/y) over the first three quarters of 2026, from 5.0% in 2025.
More worryingly, the gap is widening between weak domestic demand
and the very strong performance of exports, driven by artificial intelli-
gence equipment and green technologies. Furthermore, the dynamism

of export-oriented industries has not, so far, had any positive knock-on
effects on other sectors or on households.
Domestic demand is struggling. The property crisis, now in its sixth
year, and a sluggish labour market are weighing on household confi -
dence and consumption. Since the start of 2026, households have been
prioritising debt reduction (bank loans to households have fallen by
1% y/y), house sales have continued to fall, and retail sales have been

stagnating (Chart 1). On the investment front, the decline is becoming
more pronounced and widespread: it is affecting not only the property
sector but also infrastructure and many manufacturing sectors (with
the exception of the textile industry, transport and communications,
and electronic and electrical equipment).
Exports remain very buoyant. Exports continue to be the main driver of
growth (their net contribution is expected to account for around a third

of real GDP growth in 2026, as it did in 2024–2025). Against a backdrop
of rebound in global trade, Chinese companies maintained their market
share at 14.6% of total exports (measured in US dollars) in the first
half of 2026, as they had in 2025. Growth in goods exports accelerated
(21.9% y/y in USD over the first nine months, compared with 5.2% in
2025), driven by both volumes and prices. These have rebounded after
three years of decline, driven by rising costs of raw materials and the

prices of goods required for AI, but they have increased at a slower rate
than the global price average. Exports from high-tech sectors (batte -
ries, electric vehicles, robots and AI equipment) are posting the highest
growth rates (+55.8% y/y for all AI-related goods over the first nine
months of 2026).
Record external surpluses. Imports, which had been stagnating since
2022, have been recovering since the start of the year (up 27.2% y/y
over the first nine months), also driven by both volumes and prices.

The countries benefiting from this rebound in the Chinese market are
commodity exporters and Asian producers integrated into AI value
chains (South Korea in particular). Otherwise, China has significantly
reduced its reliance on imports over the past few years. Overall, the
trade surplus has grown at a slower rate in 2026 than in previous years.
Nevertheless, it has still reached a record high: USD1,249 billion over
the twelve months to September 2026, which is three times its 2019

level (see Chart 2).

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Meanwhile, the current account surplus is expected to approach a re -
cord level of USD 800 billion in 2026, representing nearly 4% of China’s
GDP (a ratio already seen in the late 2000s). As a proportion of global
GDP , this is unprecedented (at 0.6%).
Imbalances set to persist. The imbalances inherent in the growth mo-
del are likely to persist, fuelled notably by the strong price and non-

price competitiveness of Chinese goods. This competitiveness remains
underpinned by:
i/ innovation efforts and the move up the value chain in high-tech sec-
tors and the low-carbon transition;
ii/ low production costs (labour, low-cost credit, land and energy, tax
benefits1);
iii/ the yuan’s exchange rate. Its slow appreciation since mid-2025 has
not offset the cumulative depreciation from 2022 to 2025 (nominal and
real depreciation due to low inflation), and its real effective exchange

rate (REER) remains 10% below its 2021 average. At the end of 2025,
the IMF estimated that the REER was undervalued by between 12%
and 20%.
Beijing’s economic strategy is exacerbating imbalances. The 15th Five-
Year Plan2, launched this year, prioritises innovation, high-tech (ex -
port-oriented) sectors and technological self-reliance above all else,
whilst reforms to the social security system and private consumption
remain on the back burner (see Chart 3). Furthermore, fiscal policy,

which had provided moderate support for household spending for a
time in 2025, was tightened in 2026 (until the recent announcements
of new measures for the final quarter).
WHY BEIJING IS DIGGING ITS HEELS IN
Beijing is not giving ground on any key issues. Faced with trading
partners calling on China to rebalance its economic model, reduce in -
dustrial subsidies and strengthen the yuan, Beijing responds that the
trade surplus reflects the competitiveness of its goods, points to the

United States’ insufficient savings, and uses critical materials as a bar-
gaining chip in negotiations.
The need for growth. Exports are a key driver of growth at a time when
the Chinese economy is undergoing a period of transition. The country
has moved away from its pre-2022 model, which was based on in -
vestment in property and traditional industries and financed by debt.
Growth is now driven by ‘new productive forces’, and exports must pro-

vide producers with the market that domestic demand does not. Fur -
thermore, innovation and the roll-out of AI must stimulate productivity
gains and offset the impact of the shrinking labour force on economic
growth. It is essential to limit the slowdown of growth in order to sup-
port job creation and absorb the economy’s excess debt.
The strategic argument. Exports are a means of asserting China’s in -
dustrial and technological supremacy, weakening its competitors, and

strengthening ‘national security’. They place China at the heart of va -
lue chains in the most strategic industries, such as AI and the low-car-
bon transition. Furthermore, the pursuit of self-sufficiency and import
substitution — which exacerbate external imbalances — also help to
build national sovereignty by reducing dependencies.
A political and fiscal choice. Expanding the social safety net is seen as
a solution for providing long-term support for household consumption3

by reducing their savings rate (estimated at 35.7% of disposable income
in 2025, which is more than double the average rate for the euro area).
1 According to the IMF, the cost of China’s industrial policy amounts to about 4% of GDP . See IMF, ‘Industrial Policy in China: Quantification and Impact on Misalloca-
tion’, August 2025: https://doi.org/10.5089/9798229017855.001
2 See BNP Paribas, EcoWeek, China’s 15th Five-Year Plan: prioritising power over addressing imbalances

3 The IMF estimates, for example, that doubling social expenditure (health, education, social services and basic pensions) in rural areas could reduce savings and
raise the ratio of private consumption to GDP by 1.8 pp in the medium term, at an annual fiscal cost of 1% of GDP . See IMF, People’s Republic of China 2025 Article IV,
February 2026: https://doi.org/10.5089/9798229038911.002
4 See BNP Paribas, EcoWeek, When it comes to China, Europe should focus less on its trade deficits and more on its investments

However, this is not a priority for Beijing, certainly due to ‘ideologi -
cal’ mistrust of the welfare state and a desire for fiscal prudence.
A major reform of the healthcare or pension systems would place a
strain on public finances in both the short and long term, particularly
as the population ages. Whilst the central government remains in a
sound financial position, local governments are already under strain
following several years of falling land sales proceeds, high deficits and

rising debt. The authorities do not have a large fiscal room for ma -
noeuvre and wish to preserve it in anticipation of future shocks.
LIMITATIONS AND POSSIBLE ADJUSTMENTS
In China, a society gripped by anxiety… Economic imbalances are ta -
king their toll on households. The emphasis placed on manufacturing
rather than services is having a negative impact on employment and
wages (the share of value added allocated to employees’ pay is esti -
mated at 50% in the services sector and 41% in manufacturing). The

downturn in the property market is eroding households’ wealth, and
their savings are mainly held in low-yield bank accounts. Finally, social
spending is rising slowly (by 5.2% a year since 2020, compared with
9.7% over the previous five years).
… and enterprises under pressure. The low prices of Chinese goods,
both for export and on the domestic market — resulting from excess
production capacity and ‘involution’ — are eroding corporate profit
margins. Total profits for industrial enterprises have recovered this

year but they are highly concentrated, particularly in AI-related sec -
tors, while the proportion of industrial companies reporting losses has
continued to rise (30%, compared with 26% in 2022). These vulnerabili-
ties are having increasing contagion effects on the rest of the economy,
notably through worsening payment delays and rising pressure on the
profitability of financial institutions.
Abroad, trading partners are becoming increasingly intolerant. This

is particularly the case of the United States and the European Union 4,
which have absorbed 24% and 28% respectively of China’s trade surplus

The bank
for a changing
world
over the last twelve months (data from China’s General Administration
of Customs). Tensions between the US and China could rise again in
2027 when the current truce (which has mainly led to a reduction in
tariffs following the peak in H1 2025) comes to an end. Against a back-
drop of competition for technological supremacy and industrial sove -
reignty, the trend towards decoupling between China and the US could

continue, thereby reducing market opportunities for Chinese producers.
Europe, for its part, is reinforcing its strategic approach and expanding
its toolkit of measures, moving towards more targeted restrictions and
stricter market access conditions, especially in case of serious market
distortions). Rather than pursuing a strategy of decoupling, the EU is
seeking to reduce risks and provide greater protection for critical sec -
tors (including the automotive and chemical industries) without clo -

sing its market and whilst continuing to attract Chinese investment.
This strategy will also lead to tensions. With imbalances becoming
increasingly unsustainable, what course of action would make it pos -
sible to avoid a sudden adjustment precipitated by episodes of crises?
A ‘concerted’ adjustment is difficult to envisage. A negotiated adjust -
ment, along the lines of the 1985 Plaza agreements, seems unlikely in
the current international and military context. Furthermore, China and

its trading partners largely disagree on the diagnosis and the causes of
the imbalances. Moreover, a simple revaluation of the yuan would, on
its own, have only a limited effect on China’s competitiveness.
A gradual adjustment may be possible… in the long term. However, the
reduction of imbalances will need to come both from partner countries
(through increased savings) and from China. In the short term, China
can ease trade tensions by investing more abroad, which is all the

easier given that it has huge current account surpluses. This would
enable it to maintain its access to foreign markets and support the
production capacity of its trading partners. More structural changes
(a more flexible exchange rate regime, less fiscal support for indus -
tries and more support for households) will only be feasible once Bei -
jing considers that the costs of its export-led model will outweigh the
strategic gains. A key challenge for trading partners will be to work out

how to shift this cost-benefit balance.
Christine Peltier
christine.peltier@bnpparibas.com

⾃阐发观点。
上述分歧产⽣的直接后果已在市场上清晰呈现。美联储上⽉加息后，市场从沃什的新闻发布会
⾔辞中推断连续加息的可能性较⾼；其后两位副主席相继表态，暗⽰并不急于⽴即⾏动， 10 ⽉
加息概率随即⼤幅回落。
全国互助保险公司⾸席经济学家 Kathy Bostjancic 表⽰：
沟通路线之争：是否告知市场 " 反应函数 "
这场争论的实质，并⾮是否恢复承诺式前瞻指引 —— ⼏乎没有官员希望重回 2021 年前那种做
法，彼时过于明确的利率承诺被认为拖延了美联储对通胀的应对。
⽂章指出核⼼分歧在于：官员是否仍应向公众解释，⾃⼰将如何响应经济变化？
沃什的⽴场鲜明。他认为，持续叙述政策展望会将官员锁定在⾃⾝预测之中，同时模糊市场能
够向美联储反映的信号。他本⽉在曼⾕表⽰：
⽀持这⼀⽴场的⾥⼠满联储主席 Tom Barkin 也对记者表⽰，⾃⼰ " ⾮常乐⻅这似乎正在成为机构
惯例 " 。 Barkin ⼀贯谈论经济形势，但⼏乎从不就具体会议表态。
反对声⾳：不解释框架只会 " 制造噪⾳ "

如果沃什不开⼝，其他⼈就会填补这个空⽩。
我不打算现在就说我在今年剩余会议上可能会做什么，这是美联储的新篇章，我们不再做这种事
了。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

然⽽，多位官员公开为更充分的沟通辩护。
克利夫兰联储主席 Beth Hammack本⽉发表演讲指出，如果家庭或企业 " 在对政策⾛向存在错误
判断的情况下进⾏借贷，代价可能⼗分⾼昂 " 。
她在接受采访时强调，阐明⾃⾝思维框架并不等同于做出承诺：
圣路易斯联储主席 Alberto Musalem在伦敦的讲话中直接驳斥 " 少说话可以让市场专注于数据 " 的
逻辑，称⼀家对⾃⾝框架守⼝如瓶的央⾏ " 并不能让市场聚焦于数据，猜测只会制造噪⾳ " 。
他补充道：
Hammack 与 Musalem 均于 2024 年出任现职，两⼈均来⾃⾦融业背景，今年以来也始终是主张进
⼀步加息的代表性声⾳。
" 实验 " 遭遇现实检验，前官员提出警⽰
这场沟通实验在上⽉加息后迅速遭遇压⼒测试。
Nick Timiraos 指出，市场从沃什的表态及经济数据中推断 10 ⽉连续加息概率较⾼，其后副主席
们相继发声纠偏，暗⽰并不急于再次⾏动，市场预期随即逆转。
美联储理事 Christopher Waller 将这⼀插曲形容为⼤幅压缩前瞻指引的局限性，称此举⽆异

于 " 倒洗澡⽔时把孩⼦也⼀起倒掉 "，并直⾔：
前美联储副主席 Donald Kohn表⽰，在向市场发出如此⼤的信号之前，他过去会先把讲话内容
分享给时任主席格林斯潘或伯南克，征询意⻅。
他表⽰，两⼈从未表⽰过异议。 Kohn 说：
委员会缺乏共识，乱象或将延续
令这⼀状况更为复杂的是，当前官员们不仅对经济形势判断存在分歧，也对政策路径未能形成
统⼀叙事。
他们听到的不是承诺，因为我没有给出承诺，我是在解释⼀个框架。
摆在央⾏⾯前的选择不是在嘈杂的过度承诺与沉默寡⾔之间⼆选⼀，⽽是在让公众⾃⾏猜测与主
动告知之间做出抉择。
我想不出有什么⽐那⼀周发⽣的事情更像前瞻指引了。
当前事态表明，⾄少部分委员会成员对如此匮乏的前瞻指引感到不安，因为市场正在⾛到委员会
希望引导的⽅向之前。

Document 558: バーナム新政権も財政拡張路線を堅持

Source PDF: all report pdf/バーナム新政権も財政拡張路線を堅持.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 6 日

MURC Focus

バーナム新政権も財政拡張路線を堅持

～10 月末発表の秋季予算案で具体的な財源を示せるか～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 伝統的な財政拡張を訴えるバーナム新政権 
英国では、9 月 27 日から 30 日にかけて、北西部の主要都市リバプールで与党である労働党の党大会が開催
された。7 月 20 日に就任したアンディ・バーナム首相にとって初の党大会となると同時に、新政権の財政運営方
針が示されるという点で、この党大会は非常に高い注目を浴びた。この場で示されたのは、中道左派である労働
党らしい、伝統的な財政拡張路線に基づくプランだった。

具体的には、バーナム 新政権の関係閣僚らは、政府調達（政府が民間からモノやサービスを購入すること）の
増強による景気浮揚策を主張した。例えばジョン・ヒーリー財務相は 28 日の党大会で、スコットランドのクライド海
軍基地に浮きドック 3 基を建設する他、海洋調査船を建造することに数十億ポンドを投資する計画を発表した。
いずれも国内の業者に発注することで雇用や所得につなげる方針である。

また労働党大会に先立つ 26 日、バーナム新政権は、新築住宅を初めて購入する国民向けに新たな住宅購
入支援策を導入すると発表した。保守党政権下の 2013 年から 2023 年にかけて実施された“Help to Buy”と似
たスキームであり、購入者は購入価格の 2.5%に相当する頭金を支払えば、政府が購入価格の 20%までを融資
するというものである。

このように、バーナム新政権が目指す方針は、労働党に伝統的な“大きな政府”による財政拡張路線を反映し
ている。そうした点で、バーナム新政権の方針はキア・スターマー前政権とはあまり変化がない。一方で、新政権
が示した財政拡張路線が金融市場の評価を得るためには、財源を明示する必要がある。10 月 28 日に発表する
秋季予算案で、金融市場が納得する内容を示せるかが鍵を握る。

〇9月末にリバプールで開催された労働党の党大会で、バーナム新政権の財政運営方針が示されたが 、それ
は中道左派である労働党らしい伝統的な財政拡張路線に基づくプランだった。 
〇一方、金融市場 では、それまではバーナム 人気によって労働党 の政権運営が安定するとの思惑から“英国
買い”が先行していたが、党大会前後から調整色を濃くしている。 
〇増税は有権者の反発を、国債発行が投資家の反発を生むと予想される中、秋季予算で財政拡張の財源の
筋道をきちんと示せなければ、バーナム新政権も短命で終わりかねない。

### Page 2

2. 財政拡張を続けてきた近年の英国 
バーナム新政権は財政拡張に活路を 見いだそうとしているが、これまでも英国の政権は財政拡張に臨んでき
た（図表 1）。例えば英国の実質 GDP（国内総生産）に占める公需（＝政府支出＋公的固定資本形成）は、保守
党政権（テリーザ・メイ元首相）期の 2018 年後半に 21.5%を下回るまで低下したが、以降は上昇し、直近 2026
年 4～6 月時点で 24.4%となっている（図表 1）。

この間、2020 年 1～3 月期に生じたコロナショック後の公需の減退も限定的にとどまっており、結局は公需が
GDP に占める割合は“V 字回復”することとなった。労働党が政権を奪取したのは 2024 年 7 月の総選挙だが、
それ以前の保守党政権下でも、英国は事実上、財政拡張を続けていたことになる。その結果、公的債務残高の
対 GDP 比率も、一時は高インフレのために低下したが、再び上昇した（図表 2）。

このように近年の英国は、保革の立場を問わず財政拡張を続けてきた。しかし、EU 離脱やエネルギー不安に
伴う供給制約の高まりの中でそうした手法を取り続けたため 、英国はスタグフレーション（景気停滞と物価高進の
併存）に突入した。その改善のために必要な処方箋は、規制緩和を通じた供給の刺激である。しかし、それは“小
さな政府”を追求することにもなるため、中道左派として“大きな政府”を目指す労働党は容認できない。

バーナム新首相は地方の主導の経済成長を訴え、そうした意味での分権化に意欲的と考えられる。 ただし労
働党としては、公需による民需の刺激を通じた景気浮揚を引き続き志向していることが、秋の党大会で示された
形である。一方、金融市場では、バーナム新首相就任直後は、首相の人気に支えられて新政権の運営が安定す
るとの思惑から“英国買い”が先行していたが、党大会前後から調整色を濃くしている。

図表 1. 公需が GDP に占める割合 図表 2. 英国の公的債務残高

### Page 3

3. 財源なき財政拡張で信用を失うリスク 
スターマー前政権もまた、財政拡張路線による景気浮揚を目指した が、同時にそれは、供給の底上げを狙うも
のでもあった。老朽インフラ（鉄道や水道など）の再開発はその最たるものである。またスターマー前政権は、財政
規律にも配慮し、財源を国債発行よりも増税でカバーし、 財政拡張策の整合性を取ろう とした。財源を示したこと
を投資家は評価したが、増税に有権者が反発したため、結局、スターマー前政権は短命に終わった。

スターマー前政権下で低迷した労働党の支持率は、バーナム人気に支えられ“V 字回復”し、右派の改革党
（UK Reform ）と肉薄するに至る（図表 3）。労働党としては打ち出した目玉政策を着実に遂行し、有権者の支持
をさらに確たるものにしたいところである。とはいえ、そうした目玉政策は財政拡張を伴うものであるため、財源をど
う確保するか明確な筋道を示さなければ、金融市場の動揺を誘う。

すでに英国の長期金利（10 年国債流通利回り）は、グローバルなインフレ加速懸念に伴う世界的な金利上昇
の波に飲まれ、上昇が続いている（図表 4）。金融市場は財政拡張に対して神経を尖らせているが、秋季予算で
明確な財源が示さなければ、それを嫌気した投資家が英国債（ギルト債）の売却を進め、金利が一段と上昇し、
場合によってポンド安と株安の“トリプル安”を引き起こす可能性もある。

財政拡張の財源を増税で賄おうとすると、スターマー前政権のように有権者の支持を失いかねない。 一方、国
債発行で賄おうとすると、長期金利の押し上げ圧力になる。ゆえに、歳出の見直しで財源を賄おうとするだろうが、
簡単ではないため、具体的な筋道を示せなければ、結局は金利の上昇につながる。秋季予算で財政拡張の財
源の筋道をきちんと示せなければ、バーナム新政権も短命で終わりかねない。

債券市場總評： 非投等高息有撐
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎美國經濟具韌性，支撐年底升息預期 
9 月下旬以來，美債殖利率快速上行，主要因經濟數
據仍具韌性，加上中東情勢反覆推升油價與通膨疑
慮，牽動市場持續評估後續升息空間。雖然 8 月 PCE
與核心 PCE 低於預期，但先前公布的 9 月製造業與服
務業 PMI 大幅優於預期，第 2 季 GDP 終值上修，顯
示經濟動能仍強。但最新 9 月非農意外疲弱，新增就
業僅 2.9 萬人、失業率升至 4.2%，降低短期升息急迫
性，10 月升息機率降溫，但 12 月升息預期仍存，市
場轉向觀望 9 月 CPI 公布，10 年期美債殖利率短暫回
落後仍處高檔。 
圖 1 就業數據降溫，惟美債殖利率仍處高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
▎殖利率攀升至高位，債市同步下滑 
近日部分 Fed 官員表示，後續利率決策仍需觀察更多
數據，略為緩和市場對進一步升息的定價，帶動 10 年
期美債殖利率自高點小幅回落至 5.25%。不過，殖利
率與油價仍處高檔，限制債市修復空間。9 月下旬以
來，主要債券 YTD 報酬同步下滑，美國公債與投資級
債跌幅較深，非投等債則因存續期較短、風險偏好尚
穩，跌幅相對較輕。

圖 2 利率與油價高檔，主要債券報酬下滑

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
◼ 近期美國數據仍具韌性 ， 中東局勢推升油價與通膨疑慮 ，PCE 雖低於預期 ，但 PMI
與 GDP 仍強，非農轉弱緩和升息預期，美債殖利率仍處高檔，債市承壓。美非投
等債受高息收與中短存續期緩衝 ， 跌幅較輕 ， 殖利率升至8.3%， 處近10 年高位。 
◼ 美非投等債信用結構較過去改善，較高評級的 BB 級占比已逾半，違約率約 2%，
位於近 25 年低檔。美非投等企業 2Q 營收與 EBITDA 分別年增 10.4%、14.4%，
增速加快並創近 4 年較強表現 ， 獲利增速快於營收 ， 也帶動槓桿與償息指標改善。 
◼ 殖利率高檔震盪下，債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收成為更重要的收益來源。
美非投等債因票息較高、存續期較短，可提供一定報酬緩衝。後續可依風險承受
度，關注科技、金屬與礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯產業，並偏向 BB、短中天
期及現金流較穩定發行公司。

▎高息收提供緩衝，支撐非投等債韌性 
近期公債殖利率快速上行 ， 推升企業債殖利率至近年相
對高位，其中美國非投資等級債到期殖利率已來到約
8.3%，顯著高於過去 10 年平均，殖利率水準已具備相
當吸引力。 
圖 3 美非投等債殖利率攀升至 8.3%，10 年相對高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/1

觀察近 6 個月各類債券報酬來源 ， 今年美國公債殖利率
攀升，使債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收重新成為影
響總報酬的關鍵來源。其中，美國非投資等級債因具備
較高殖利率與中短存續期，且息收水準相對較高，較能
緩衝利率上行對價格的影響， 因此相較公債與投等債，
回落幅度相對有限。

圖 4 利息收益貢獻為債券報酬主要來源

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/30

▎美經濟相對穩健 ， 非投等BB 比重提升 
從已開發國家 GDP 年增率來看，美國、歐元區與日本
未來幾季仍維持正成長， 其中美國成長表現相對穩健 ，
預估仍維持約 2%左右增速 ， 顯示高利率與油價波動下 ，
經濟仍具一定韌性。

圖 5 美國經濟動能仍相對歐元區、日本較具韌性

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/26

信用結構方面， 美國非投等債市場債信結構已較金融海
嘯前改善，BB 評級占比由 2007 年 12 月的 32%升至
2026 年 5 月的 54%，目前占整體非投等債比重過半，
CCC 與未評級占比則由 15%降至 7%。 由於BB 是非投
等債中評級最高的一層，信用風險低於 B 與 CCC，BB
級占比提高代表目前美國非投等債市場中 ，信用體質較
接近投資等級的發行公司占比明顯提升。 
圖 6 美國非投等債各信用評級占比與違約率

資料來源：JPM，2026/8/31

### Page 3

3

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
違約率方面，目前美國非投等債整體違約率約 2%，落
在歷史區間相對低位 ， 低評級債違約壓力也未見明顯擴
散。在美國經濟仍具韌性、非投等債 BB 比重提升且違
約率受控的背景下 ， 美國非投等債的高殖利率仍具基本
面支撐。

▎美非投等企業，2Q 營收獲利顯著改善 
檢視美國非投等債企業第 2 季基本面，營收與 EBITDA
延續正成長動能，且2Q26 成長幅度進一步加速。美國
非投等債企業營收季增 9.2%、 年增10.4%，EBITDA 季
增 19.2%、 年增14.4%， 營收與EBITDA 年增率雙雙創
下 4 年來最高，顯示企業營運表現仍具韌性。

圖 7  美非投等企業營收年增 10.4%，4 年來最快增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

從產業表現來看，今年商品、能源與企業投資需求仍延
續，金屬與礦業、能源、科技、零售與服務業等產業對
整體營收與 EBITDA 成長形成貢獻，使 2Q 基本面改善
具備一定廣度。EBITDA 增速高於營收增速，代表企業
獲利擴張速度快於收入成長，反映成本控管、營運效率
或價格轉嫁能力仍在。 
圖 8 EBITDA 年增顯著提升至 14%，4 年來較強增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15 
▎美非投等槓桿回落，償息能力改善 
2Q 美國非投等債企業信用指標出現改善，槓桿率降至 
4.51 倍 ， 主要反映EBITDA 成長速度快於債務增加 ， 帶
動槓桿指標修復。

圖 9 美非投等企業槓桿率回落至 4.51 倍

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

償息能力方面，儘管利息費用仍在增加，但 EBITDA 成
長仍帶動利息保障倍數回升至 4.12 倍，2 年較高水準 ，
顯示企業獲利對利息支出的覆蓋能力有所改善 ， 高利率
環境下，美非投等企業的償息能力仍相對有撐。整體來
看，2Q26 美國非投等債企業營收、獲利與信用指標同
步改善，基本面韌性仍在。

圖 10 美非投等企業利息保障倍數升至 2 年高位

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

### Page 4

4

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎非投等具高息收優勢 ， 關注BB 短天期 
近期公債殖利率仍在高檔波動 ， 美非投等債短線表現受
到壓抑。不過，經過利率重新定價後，美非投等債殖利
率已回到相對具吸引力的水準 ， 較高息收與相對較短存
續期，仍可在利率波動環境下提供一定報酬緩衝。 
綜合前述，美國經濟相對歐日仍較穩健，企業營收、獲
利與信用指標同步改善 ， 為美非投等債後續表現提供基
本面基礎。產業面可留意科技、基礎工業相關的金屬與
礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯的方向。整體而言，後
續可偏向 BB、短中天期及現金流較穩定的發行公司，
參與較高息收帶來的收益機會。

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 观点 
◼ 周度 ECI 指数：从周度数据来看，截至 2026 年 10 月 25 日，本周 ECI
供给指数为 50.19%，较上周环比回升 0.01 个百分点；ECI 需求指数为
49.93%，较上周环比回落 0.01 个百分点。 从分项来看，ECI 投资指数为
49.95%，较上周环比回落 0.01 个百分点；ECI 消费指数为 49.75%，较
上周环比持平；ECI 出口指数为 50.15%，较上周环比回落 0.02 个百分
点。 
◼ 月度 ECI 指数：从 10 月前三周的高频数据来看， ECI 供给指数为
50.18%，较 9 月回升 0.02 个百分点；ECI 需求指数为 49.94%，较 9 月
环比持平。从分项来看，ECI 投资指数为 49.96%，较 9 月回落 0.02 个
百分点；ECI 消费指数为 49.76%，较 9 月回落 0.01 个百分点；ECI 出
口指数为 50.17%，较 9 月回升持平。 
◼ 经济高频数据： 
➢ 生产：高油价对 PTA 产业链仍有扰动。本周汽车轮胎开工率延续节后
修复的趋势， 但受汽车零售景气度偏低的影响较去年同期仍偏弱； 而受
油价震荡上行影响，PTA 产业链再度受到扰动，PTA 开工率连续三周环
比回落。 上周行业则整体保持平稳运行，10 月份焦炉开工率和高炉开工
率较 9 月环比变化均不大。 
➢ 消费：乘用车零售仍是消费回暖的主要制约。从社零数据来看，汽车零
售同比降幅继续扩大至 9 月的-21.2%，而其他类别零售较低点都已经显
著改善， 指向乘用车零售仍是消费回暖的主要制约。服务消费方面，本
周地铁客运量、 航班执飞率和票房收入均略弱于去年同期， 但仍保持较
高景气度。 
➢ 地产：仍需关注地产销售修复的持续性。本周（10 月 17 日至 10 月 23
日） 主要城市新房和二手房销售均有所回落，其中三十大中城市商品房
成交面积录得 148.2 万平方米， 环比回落6.7%， 同比回落26.2%，而18
城二手房商品房成交面积录得 188.0 万平方米，环比回落 35.6%，同比
回落 24.4%。在上半月地产销售出现边际改善后，近期地产销售景气度
再度回落，仍需关注后续地产销售修复的持续性。 
➢ 出口：海运价格指数涨势有所放缓。10 月份以来， 随着全球海运进入淡
季， 集装箱运价指数和波罗的海干散货指数涨势均显著放缓， 但考虑到
地缘冲突事件仍有较高不确定性， 高油价下四季度海运价格或难以出现
明显回落。 
➢ 物价：原油价格录得近 7 个月新高。国内方面， 本周猪肉和蔬菜价格延
续季节性回落，同比也仍低于去年同期，预计 10 月份食品价格仍将录
得同比负增长； 国际方面， 近期美伊冲突风险加剧再度推升油价，10 月
23 日原油价格盘中最高触及 117.45 美元/桶，录得近 7 个月新高，或使
我国 10 月份 PPI 延续录得高位。 
➢ 整体来看，本周公布的经济数据和高频数据均指向消费、 投资和地产端
出现了一定边际改善， 但改善的持续性仍待进一步观察， 且经济增长的
结构性特征仍然突出， 供给端仍是经济修复的主要动能。而从经济增长
的中长期结构变化来看， 目前地产链条贡献正逐步下降， 出口升级、 服
务消费和非地产投资正在成为新的稳定器，即我国也正经历“去地产化
增长”。 
◼ 风险提示：美国货币政策仍有不确定性；政策出台力度低于市场预期；
房地产改善的持续性待观察。

分项来看，ECI 投资指数为 49.95%，较上周环比回落 0.01 个百分点；ECI 消费指数为

2.   本周高频数据概览 
2.1.   工业生产：高油价仍对 PTA 开工率造成压制 
开工率方面， 本周主要行业开工率各有升降。 其中本周汽车全/半钢胎开工率为 57.70%
和 64.78%， 分别环比回升 5.48 个百分点和 4.35 个百分点；本周 PTA开工率录得 67.63%，
环比回落 2.30 个百分点，较 2025 年同期回落 10.75 个百分点；本周钢厂高炉开工率录
得 80.40%，环比回落 0.09 个百分点，较 2025 年同期回落 4.33 个百分点。 
库存和产量方面，本周六港口炼焦煤库存合计 297.70 万吨，环比回升 29.71 万吨；
本周主要钢厂建筑钢材库存录得 209.35 万吨，环比回落 8.76 万吨；本周建筑钢材企业
螺纹钢产量录得 174.73 万吨，环比回升 0.84 万吨。 
负荷率方面，10 月 16 日沿海七省电厂负荷率均值录得 75.14%，环比回落 1.86 个
百分点，较 2025 年同期回落 4.14 个百分点。

2.2.   消费：节后服务消费景气度有所回落

回落 16054 辆。根据乘联会发布的最新数据，10 月 1-18 日乘用车市场零售录得 78.9 万
辆，同比 2025 年同期下降 26.0%，环比上月同期增长 2.0%，其中新能源车市场零售录
得 52.0 万辆，同比 2025 年同期下降 13.0%，环比上月同期下降 2.0%。

0.01 亿元；本周观影人次录得594.63 万人，环比回落 268.86 万人，较 2025 年同期回落
2.67 万人。

3.   本周政策一览 
表8：本周（10 月 19 日-10 月 25 日）政策梳理 
时间 部门 政策/会议 内容 
2026/10/19 央行 金融街论坛年会 
即将推动出台的一系列政策措施，包括设立应对非银机构大额
赎回的流动性支持工具、扩大常备借贷便利的适用机构范围、
推动债券市场互联互通落地、便利境外金融机构在境内外汇市
场开展人民币购售业务等。货币政策框架转型方面，央行将研
究择机把公开市场主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，公开市场
隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率。推动贷款利率定价基
准更加多元，并退出存贷款基准利率。央行将密切跟踪研判人
工智能发展的宏观影响，前瞻性做好政策考量和准备，稳妥推
进人工智能在金融领域的应用。 
2026/10/19 证监会 金融街论坛年会 
宣布高质量建设资本市场系列措施，包括同意首批 18 只主动
ETF 注册、择机修订发布上市公司证券发行注册管理办法及配
套规则、加快推动出台上市公司监管条例、支持证券行业并购
重组和后续整合、筹建基金业运行监测中心、发文推动 AI 合规
应用、提升 QFII 投资运作便利度、加快推进人民币股票交易柜
台和 REITs 纳入沪深港通等。 
2026/10/20 国家发改委   
将会同有关部门及时出台实施增量政策，推动存量政策与增量
政策协同发力，确保经济运行保持在合理区间。正在研究制定
扩大内需战略实施方案（2026-2030 年） ，并将加快推动印发实
施；合理提高公共服务支出占财政支出比重；加强新型政策性
金融工具对民间投资项目的支持力度；进一步优化实施全链条
推动重点产业发展、优化算力设施布局等政策。 
2026/10/21 
中共中央办公
厅、国务院办
公厅 
《关于推动科技创新和产业
创新深度融合的意见》 
要求推动全链条贯通、全领域覆盖、全要素融通、全方位保
障，形成科技创新和产业创新互融共促新格局。意见提出，推
动集成电路、工业母机、高端仪器、先进材料、生物制造等重
点领域关键核心技术攻关取得决定性突破。在新业态新领域开
展放宽市场准入试点，探索开展承诺即入制。加强新技术应用
监管，探索“沙盒监管”方式。

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6 至 12 个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数， 新三板基准指数为三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50 指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相
对评级体系， 表示投资的相对比重建议。 投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况， 如具体投资目的、 财务状况以及特定需求等， 并完整理解和使用本报告内容， 不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

聯茂 (6213 TT)

獲利表現令人驚豔

維持買進評等，目標價調升至 1,055 元，潛在上漲空間 41%。 
1. 自結 9 月 EPS3.54 元，遠高於市場預期。 
2. 估計 4Q26 單季合併營收季增 33.2%。 
3. 樂觀預期至 2027 年中下旬價格調漲動作都還會持續進行。

自結 9 月 EPS3.54 元，遠高於市場預期 
應主要機關要求，10/23 日盤後公告 9 月營運自結數，單月合併營收 58.24 億元，
YOY+110.7%，稅前淨利 19.18 億元，YOY+931.2%，稅後淨利 12.86 億元，
YOY+1,069.1%，EPS 3.54 元。由於獲利數字以本業為主，富邦推估單月毛利率達
38%以上，優於原預期。加計先前 8 月自結數 EPS 2.54 元，8~9 月合計 EPS 6.08
元，達我們 3Q26 單季預估值 7.62 元的 79.8%。而營收、獲利大幅成長主要來自
漲價效益及 AI 相關需求成長。富邦再次上調 3Q26 預估數，單季合併營收 156.17
億元 ，QOQ+35.5%，YOY+102.2%， 毛利率33%， 稅後EPS 8.06 元，QOQ+129.1%，
YOY+802.2%。

估計 4Q26 單季合併營收季增 33.2% 
我們原預期 4Q26 價格調漲 20%，唯以目前 E-glass 玻纖布市況緊缺來看，公司表
示價格調漲節奏由季改為月動態調整，我們預期累計季平均漲幅有機會達 25~30%，
另產能 UTR 方面，配合的玻纖布廠小量產能釋放，UTR 有機會往 80%以上走，與
原預期不變，考量上述變化，富邦將 4Q26 季營運成長幅度上調至 30~35%，推估
單季合併營收 208.09 億元，QOQ+33.2%，YOY+133.4%，毛利率 40.4%，EPS 
13.86 元，QOQ+72%，YOY+1,076.6%。

樂觀預期至 2027 年中下旬價格調漲動作都還會持續進行 
聯茂 10/20~10/22 日於 TPCA 展場表示，目前原料緊缺格局不變，今年 3 月以來
多次進行價格調漲，累計已有五成以上，且 4Q26 將持續反應市場需求及成本進行
價格調漲。展望 2027 年，由於短期 E-glass 緊缺不變，加上接下來高階銅箔也呈
供不應求，因此公司以就目前市況來看，預期至 2027 年中下旬價格調漲動作都還
會持續進行。至於市場擔憂 4Q26~1H27 陸廠有些玻纖紗產能開出，公司表示，未
來即便陸廠有些產能開出，但關鍵產出卡在織布機。此外，除豐田自動織機外，未
來陸廠新增織布機能否符合良率及產出品質還是有待觀察。整體來看，公司對此波
漲價循環周期時間釋出更為正向看法。

富邦不同之處 
富邦 2026 及 2027 年 EPS 預估值 26.3 及 70.38 元，遠優於市場同業 17.58 及
34.04 元的平均估計值。

評價與風險 
9 月自結數反應毛利率表現優於預期，且 4Q26 續漲幅度將優於我們原預期，我們
維持買進投資評等，目標價調升至 1,055 元，約相當2027 年 15 倍 P/E。潛在風險
包括 1) 一般伺服器需求回落。2)後續價格調漲幅度不如預期，或漲價週期縮短。

產業評等 定義 
優於大盤 預估該產業在未來6個月內會比大盤指數表現突出 
持平 預估該產業在未來6個月內與大盤指數表現相較持平

Fubon's Rating System 
Rating Definition

Sector Rating Definition 
Overweight Sector expected to outperform the broader market indexes within the next 6-12 months

Document 726: 报道：AI融资弄潮儿Pimco见好就收，砍半Meta和甲骨文债券仓位，躲过大跌

Source PDF: all report pdf/报道：AI融资弄潮儿Pimco见好就收，砍半Meta和甲骨文债券仓位，躲过大跌.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

⽬前 AI 债券全线承压，此前太平洋投资管理公司（ Pimco ）精准逃顶。 Pimco 曾参与 Meta
路易斯安那州数据中⼼项⽬融资，交易规模 180 亿美元，买⼊后六⾄⼋个⽉内抛售了约 30
亿美元；还在甲⻣⽂密歇根数据中⼼融资中投⼊约 100 亿美元。 Pimco ⽬前两笔仓位均已减
持约 50% ，减持时点均在债券价格较⾼位时完成。同时 Pimco 对今年博通、软银等数桩⼤
型 AI 融资交易选择回避，表明其对 AI 债市趋于谨慎。
摘要：
全球最⼤固定收益资产管理机构之⼀太平洋投资管理公司（ Pimco ）在⼈⼯智能基础设施债券热
潮中⼤举押注，随后通过⼀系列时机精准的减持操作，在市场价格⼤幅回落前锁定盈利，成功
规避了困扰同类投资者的巨额账⾯亏损。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露，Pimco 在参与 AI 债券市场的同时，已⼤幅削减旗下最⼤仓位，部分
持仓建⽴仅数⽉便完成出售。
该公司对今年以来数桩⼤型 AI 融资交易选择回避，包括博通与 Anthropic 联合发⾏的⼤额债券，
以及软银集团约 110 亿美元的垃圾债。上述动作表明，这家债券巨头对 AI 债券市场的态度已明显

趋于审慎。
⽬前，⽀撑甲⻣⽂密歇根数据中⼼园区的相关债券已从发⾏价 98.7 美分跌⾄约 88 美分。博通等
其他⼤型 AI 债券近期同样承压，报价约为 96 美分。
多重价格下⾏信号之下， AI 债券市场的⻛险正引发业界⼴泛关注，部分投资者已将此轮热潮与互
联⽹泡沫时代相提并论。
锚定投资者优势，两笔旗舰交易均实现盈利出局
Pimco 最具代表性的 AI 债券投资，是 2024 年参与了 Meta 平台公司路易斯安那州⼤型数据中⼼项
⽬的融资 —— 该笔交易规模约 180 亿美元，被市场称为 "Beignet" ，并成为此后众多 AI 数据中⼼融
资的参照模板。
Pimco 于买⼊后六⾄⼋个⽉内抛售了约 30 亿美元，彼时该债券交易价格⾼于⾯值，初期⼀度涨
⾄ 110 美分以上。
随后， Pimco ⼜在甲⻣⽂密歇根数据中⼼园区融资中投⼊约 100 亿美元。
据知情⼈⼠透露， Pimco 作为上述两笔交易的锚定投资者，以明显低于官⽅发⾏价的折扣价认
购，从⼀开始便为⾃⾝设置了安全垫。监管⽂件显⽰， Pimco ⽬前两笔仓位均已减持约 50% ，

减持时点均在债券价格较⾼位时完成。
根据⾏业价格报告系统 Trace 的数据，甲⻣⽂密歇根州债券⽬前已回落⾄约 88 美分，较发⾏价⼤
幅下滑。 Pimco 尽管仍是这两笔交易的最⼤持有⼈之⼀，但⼤规模套现已使其整体⻛险敞⼝显著
收窄。
主动回避多宗⼤型交易，对 AI 债市转趋保守
在成功锁定前期收益的同时， Pimco 今年明显加⼤了对新增 AI 债券投资的筛选⼒度。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
